Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. Like, I’m in this apprenticeship and I keep thinking I’m not actually supposed to be there, like I’ve somehow fooled people into thinking I can do it, and eventually they’re going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.

And it’s not even always over huge things, which is part of why I feel kind of stupid saying it out loud. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.

It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. Not just a bad week here and there. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.

At first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.

Like my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.

And volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.

It’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.

death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have some plan to hurt myself, but when I'm driving home after class or lying awake I get these thoughts that it would be easier if I just didn't wake up tomorrow.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: There were weeks where I felt almost too good, like Miami was mine and every little comment from a coworker who slowed me down made me snap way harder than I normally would.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I had this nonstop charge in my body where after a full day on pipes I would still go to evening class, reorganize my tools, clean my apartment, and start drawing systems instead of sitting down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I honestly thought I could design something better than the instructors and the senior guys, like I was about to change how every building in Miami handled water if people would just listen.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: In class and at the shop I would start explaining an idea and just keep going, talking over people and barely taking a breath even when I could see they were trying to get back to work.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My head would jump from pipe layouts to a business plan to texting classmates to fixing my mom's bathroom, all at once, and I couldn't slow it down enough to pick one thing.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I signed myself up to help classmates, volunteered for extra design sketches, messaged old contacts about side projects, and took on more than I could finish even though part of me knew the hours didn't add up.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: My instructors and foreman have had to chase me about unfinished sketches and slower work, and I’m scared I’m close to damaging the apprenticeship and certification I worked hard to get.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 5 months\n  manifestation: This whole pattern has been going on for about five months, starting after I began the stretch has been with me for about nine months, though it became harder to hide once school and work started slipping.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 20 / plumber_pipefitter_or_steamfitter / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S030\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. Like, I’m in this apprenticeship and I keep thinking I’m not actually supposed to be there, like I’ve somehow fooled people into thinking I can do it, and eventually they’re going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.\n\nAnd it’s not even always over huge things, which is part of why I feel kind of stupid saying it out loud. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of inadequacy and self-doubt?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.

It’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.

Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 20 / plumber_pipefitter_or_steamfitter / associates\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Worthlessness_Guilt\nmanifestation: \"What made me come in is that I keep feeling like I ruined things for everybody, like I'm a fake in the apprenticeship and I let my family and the younger apprentices down over stuff I know other people would probably just fix and move on from.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.

Like with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.

And it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.

I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 20 / plumber_pipefitter_or_steamfitter / associates\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Death_Suicide\nmanifestation: \"I don't have some plan to hurt myself, but when I'm driving home after class or lying awake I get these thoughts that it would be easier if I just didn't wake up tomorrow.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.

But I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.

I know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.

I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.

But there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.

So, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.

I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.

Same with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.

And I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. Like, I’m in this apprenticeship and I keep thinking I’m not actually supposed to be there, like I’ve somehow fooled people into thinking I can do it, and eventually they’re going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.\n\nAnd it’s not even always over huge things, which is part of why I feel kind of stupid saying it out loud. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of inadequacy and self-doubt?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. Not just a bad week here and there. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.\n\nAt first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.

I keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.

And it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.

So, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.

I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.\n\nAt first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.

It’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.

And then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. So yeah… maybe “worthless” sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what it feels like some days. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.

Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. So yeah… maybe “worthless” sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what it feels like some days. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s timed perfectly like that. But mornings can definitely be when it starts, especially if I wake up and remember I have to go in to the apprenticeship or deal with people who are expecting me to be competent.

It’s like before anything has even happened, I’ll already be thinking, “What am I going to mess up today?” And then it turns into this bigger feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody somehow, or that I’m just pretending to belong there. Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.

And then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.

So… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. Like, I’m in this apprenticeship and I keep thinking I’m not actually supposed to be there, like I’ve somehow fooled people into thinking I can do it, and eventually they’re going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.\n\nAnd it’s not even always over huge things, which is part of why I feel kind of stupid saying it out loud. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of inadequacy and self-doubt?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. Not just a bad week here and there. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.\n\nAt first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. So yeah… maybe “worthless” sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what it feels like some days. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a disappointment to others every morning when you wake up?\n\nPatient: Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s timed perfectly like that. But mornings can definitely be when it starts, especially if I wake up and remember I have to go in to the apprenticeship or deal with people who are expecting me to be competent.\n\nIt’s like before anything has even happened, I’ll already be thinking, “What am I going to mess up today?” And then it turns into this bigger feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody somehow, or that I’m just pretending to belong there. Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.\n\nAnd then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. It’s more like there are days where nothing really pokes at it as hard, so I can get through the day without spiraling on it the whole time.

But even on those days, it feels like it’s kind of sitting in the background. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. But then if I do one thing wrong, even something small that other people would probably just fix and move on from, it comes right back — that feeling that I’m a fake there, that I don’t actually belong, and that I’ve somehow ruined things for everybody.

And it turns into this whole guilt thing, like I’m letting my family down because they were proud of me, and even letting the younger apprentices down, which I know sounds like a lot to put on myself. So… yes, I have better days, but I don’t know if I have many days where I feel totally free of it. It’s more like the volume gets turned down for a while.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. Like, I’m in this apprenticeship and I keep thinking I’m not actually supposed to be there, like I’ve somehow fooled people into thinking I can do it, and eventually they’re going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.\n\nAnd it’s not even always over huge things, which is part of why I feel kind of stupid saying it out loud. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of inadequacy and self-doubt?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. Not just a bad week here and there. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.\n\nAt first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a disappointment to others every morning when you wake up?\n\nPatient: Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no.
