Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.\n\nAnd then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nSo… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel worthless or like a disappointment to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. It’s more like there are days where nothing really pokes at it as hard, so I can get through the day without spiraling on it the whole time.\n\nBut even on those days, it feels like it’s kind of sitting in the background. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nSo… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel worthless or like a disappointment to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. It’s more like there are days where nothing really pokes at it as hard, so I can get through the day without spiraling on it the whole time.\n\nBut even on those days, it feels like it’s kind of sitting in the background. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. But then if I do one thing wrong, even something small that other people would probably just fix and move on from, it comes right back — that feeling that I’m a fake there, that I don’t actually belong, and that I’ve somehow ruined things for everybody.\n\nAnd it turns into this whole guilt thing, like I’m letting my family down because they were proud of me, and even letting the younger apprentices down, which I know sounds like a lot to put on myself. So… yes, I have better days, but I don’t know if I have many days where I feel totally free of it. It’s more like the volume gets turned down for a while.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… definitely. That’s actually been one of the things that makes me feel weird, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking them, I just sort of… stopped reaching for them.

Like my sketchbook, for example. I used to always have it nearby, and even if I was tired I’d still doodle or plan something out, because it felt like mine, you know? Like something that made me feel more like myself outside of work and everything. But lately I can literally walk past it sitting there and just not feel anything pulling me toward it. Not even guilt sometimes, just blank.

And volleyball too. I used to look forward to playing, even just casually, because it got me out of my head. Now if there’s a chance to go, I’ll skip it or tell myself I’m too tired or it’s too much effort, but it’s not only being tired. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it enough to make myself go.

Same with the volunteer group texts. Those used to make me feel connected, like I was doing something good and being part of something. Now I see the messages pop up and I just ignore them, and I don’t even have a strong feeling about it in the moment. It’s kind of scary how little I feel about things that used to matter to me.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of inadequacy and self-doubt?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. Not just a bad week here and there. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.\n\nAt first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. So yeah… maybe “worthless” sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what it feels like some days. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a disappointment to others every morning when you wake up?\n\nPatient: Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s timed perfectly like that. But mornings can definitely be when it starts, especially if I wake up and remember I have to go in to the apprenticeship or deal with people who are expecting me to be competent.\n\nIt’s like before anything has even happened, I’ll already be thinking, “What am I going to mess up today?” And then it turns into this bigger feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody somehow, or that I’m just pretending to belong there. Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.\n\nAnd then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nSo… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel worthless or like a disappointment to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. It’s more like there are days where nothing really pokes at it as hard, so I can get through the day without spiraling on it the whole time.\n\nBut even on those days, it feels like it’s kind of sitting in the background. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. But then if I do one thing wrong, even something small that other people would probably just fix and move on from, it comes right back — that feeling that I’m a fake there, that I don’t actually belong, and that I’ve somehow ruined things for everybody.\n\nAnd it turns into this whole guilt thing, like I’m letting my family down because they were proud of me, and even letting the younger apprentices down, which I know sounds like a lot to put on myself. So… yes, I have better days, but I don’t know if I have many days where I feel totally free of it. It’s more like the volume gets turned down for a while.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually been one of the things that makes me feel weird, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking them, I just sort of… stopped reaching for them.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to always have it nearby, and even if I was tired I’d still doodle or plan something out, because it felt like mine, you know? Like something that made me feel more like myself outside of work and everything. But lately I can literally walk past it sitting there and just not feel anything pulling me toward it. Not even guilt sometimes, just blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too. I used to look forward to playing, even just casually, because it got me out of my head. Now if there’s a chance to go, I’ll skip it or tell myself I’m too tired or it’s too much effort, but it’s not only being tired. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it enough to make myself go.\n\nSame with the volunteer group texts. Those used to make me feel connected, like I was doing something good and being part of something. Now I see the messages pop up and I just ignore them, and I don’t even have a strong feeling about it in the moment. It’s kind of scary how little I feel about things that used to matter to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It feels close to every day, yeah. I don’t know if I’d say every single day in the exact same way or at the exact same level, because some days I can keep moving and not get stuck on it as hard. But it’s there a lot.

That’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I keep getting this feeling like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when the thing that happened is probably something small that other people would just fix and move on from. At the apprenticeship, especially, I’ll make a mistake or even think I might have made one, and then my head goes straight to, “I’m a fake, I don’t belong here, everyone’s going to realize I shouldn’t be doing this.”

And then it spreads out past just work. I start thinking about my family and how they were proud of me, and it feels like I’m letting them down. And even with the younger apprentices, I feel like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things or set a decent example, and instead I feel like I’m failing them too. I know that probably sounds like I’m making myself responsible for way too much, but in the moment it feels really true. So… maybe not every single day perfectly, but most days, yeah, that worthless-disappointment feeling is there in some form.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess... I came in because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up for everybody. Like, I’m in this apprenticeship and I keep thinking I’m not actually supposed to be there, like I’ve somehow fooled people into thinking I can do it, and eventually they’re going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought.\n\nAnd it’s not even always over huge things, which is part of why I feel kind of stupid saying it out loud. It’ll be stuff that other people would probably just fix, learn from, and move on from, but I’ll sit there thinking, “Great, now I’ve let everyone down.” My family, the people who believed in me, even the younger apprentices who kind of look to me sometimes—I feel like I’m disappointing all of them. It just keeps looping in my head, like I ruined things or I’m a fake, and I don’t know how to get out from under that feeling.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of inadequacy and self-doubt?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say around nine months, if I’m being honest. Not just a bad week here and there. It sort of settled in and stayed, like this heavy background thing where I kept doubting myself and feeling like I wasn’t measuring up.\n\nAt first I could kind of cover it, or at least I thought I could. I’d tell myself I was just tired or stressed and that everyone feels unsure sometimes. But once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to hide from other people—and from myself, I guess. That’s when it felt less like normal nerves and more like something I couldn’t just talk myself out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s actually one of the things that feels weirdly scary to admit, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking things. It’s more like they’re still sitting there, but I don’t really… reach for them anymore.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to draw all the time, even if it was just little rough sketches after work or class, and it made me feel like I was still me outside of the apprenticeship stuff. Now I can walk right past it on my desk for days and just feel nothing about it. Not even guilt sometimes, just kind of blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too—I used to really look forward to playing, seeing people, moving around, joking between games. Lately I’ll skip it and it doesn’t even feel like I’m missing something fun. Same with this volunteer group I’m in. Their texts come through and I just ignore them, even though I used to be the kind of person who answered right away and actually wanted to help.\n\nIt’s like the things that used to make me feel like myself are still there, but I’m not connected to them the same way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly, at least not all the time. Sometimes it’s more like there’s this dull, hollow feeling, like I’m just kind of moving through the day without much in me. Other times I do feel down, but it’s not always crying or anything obvious like that.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be sitting there and realize I haven’t really felt anything good for a while, or I’m just quiet inside, if that makes sense. Empty is probably a fair word for it, but I guess I hesitate because I keep thinking, “Well, other people probably have it worse,” so maybe I shouldn’t describe it that way. But yeah, there are definitely times where I feel kind of sad or blank inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past nine months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight closely or anything, so I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know.\n\nMy clothes haven’t been, like, drastically falling off me or suddenly not fitting, at least not that I’ve noticed. I mean, there are days where I look in the mirror and think I seem different, but I honestly can’t tell if that’s real or just me being in my head about everything. I don’t think there’s been a significant amount of weight loss, though—not that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or that you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I don’t know if “not a good person” is exactly the way I’d say it every time, but I get stuck in this loop where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when part of me knows that sounds dramatic.\n\nLike with the apprenticeship, I keep feeling like I’m fake somehow, like I’m just pretending I belong there and eventually everyone’s going to realize I’m not as capable as they thought. And then if I mess something up, even something that other people would probably just fix and move on from, I turn it into this whole thing in my head—like I let everyone down, like I made more work for people, like I’m the reason things got harder.\n\nAnd it’s not just at work. I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. So yeah… maybe “worthless” sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what it feels like some days. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a disappointment to others every morning when you wake up?\n\nPatient: Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s timed perfectly like that. But mornings can definitely be when it starts, especially if I wake up and remember I have to go in to the apprenticeship or deal with people who are expecting me to be competent.\n\nIt’s like before anything has even happened, I’ll already be thinking, “What am I going to mess up today?” And then it turns into this bigger feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody somehow, or that I’m just pretending to belong there. Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.\n\nAnd then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nSo… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel worthless or like a disappointment to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. It’s more like there are days where nothing really pokes at it as hard, so I can get through the day without spiraling on it the whole time.\n\nBut even on those days, it feels like it’s kind of sitting in the background. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. But then if I do one thing wrong, even something small that other people would probably just fix and move on from, it comes right back — that feeling that I’m a fake there, that I don’t actually belong, and that I’ve somehow ruined things for everybody.\n\nAnd it turns into this whole guilt thing, like I’m letting my family down because they were proud of me, and even letting the younger apprentices down, which I know sounds like a lot to put on myself. So… yes, I have better days, but I don’t know if I have many days where I feel totally free of it. It’s more like the volume gets turned down for a while.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually been one of the things that makes me feel weird, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking them, I just sort of… stopped reaching for them.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to always have it nearby, and even if I was tired I’d still doodle or plan something out, because it felt like mine, you know? Like something that made me feel more like myself outside of work and everything. But lately I can literally walk past it sitting there and just not feel anything pulling me toward it. Not even guilt sometimes, just blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too. I used to look forward to playing, even just casually, because it got me out of my head. Now if there’s a chance to go, I’ll skip it or tell myself I’m too tired or it’s too much effort, but it’s not only being tired. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it enough to make myself go.\n\nSame with the volunteer group texts. Those used to make me feel connected, like I was doing something good and being part of something. Now I see the messages pop up and I just ignore them, and I don’t even have a strong feeling about it in the moment. It’s kind of scary how little I feel about things that used to matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every single day?\n\nPatient: It feels close to every day, yeah. I don’t know if I’d say every single day in the exact same way or at the exact same level, because some days I can keep moving and not get stuck on it as hard. But it’s there a lot.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I keep getting this feeling like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when the thing that happened is probably something small that other people would just fix and move on from. At the apprenticeship, especially, I’ll make a mistake or even think I might have made one, and then my head goes straight to, “I’m a fake, I don’t belong here, everyone’s going to realize I shouldn’t be doing this.”\n\nAnd then it spreads out past just work. I start thinking about my family and how they were proud of me, and it feels like I’m letting them down. And even with the younger apprentices, I feel like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things or set a decent example, and instead I feel like I’m failing them too. I know that probably sounds like I’m making myself responsible for way too much, but in the moment it feels really true. So… maybe not every single day perfectly, but most days, yeah, that worthless-disappointment feeling is there in some form.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… usually it’s not just like a quick thought that pops up and then leaves. It can hang around for a lot of the day, especially if something at the apprenticeship sets it off early.

Like if I mess something up, or even just think I did, it’s not over once the actual mistake is fixed. Other people might just correct it and move on, but I’ll keep replaying it in my head for hours. I’ll be working and still thinking, “You don’t belong here, you’re faking it, everyone’s going to figure out you shouldn’t be here.” And then it turns into this bigger thing where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when I know logically that sounds dramatic.

It’s not always at full volume the entire day, I guess. Sometimes I’m distracted enough to get through tasks. But it’s like it stays underneath everything. I’ll think about my family being proud of me and feel like I’m letting them down, and then I think about the younger apprentices too, like I’m supposed to be someone steady or useful and instead I’m just disappointing people.

So… yeah, most of the day a lot of the time. Maybe not every single minute, but it follows me around once it’s there.

I think about my family too, and the younger apprentices, and I feel like I’m supposed to be setting some kind of example or at least not being a burden. But instead I’ll sit there thinking, “You’re disappointing everyone. You’re not who they think you are.” It’s embarrassing to say out loud because I know people make mistakes, and if someone else told me this I’d probably tell them they’re being way too hard on themselves. But when it’s me, it feels true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t really like saying that, because it sounds more serious than how I mean it in my head, maybe, and I don’t want you to think I have some whole plan or that I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself.\n\nBut I do get thoughts like… when I’m driving home after class, especially if it’s quiet and I’m tired, I’ll think, “It would be easier if I just didn’t have to do this anymore.” Or when I’m lying awake at night, it’s more like, “What if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow?” Not in a dramatic way, exactly. More like this exhausted thought that everything would stop and I wouldn’t have to keep messing things up or feeling like this.\n\nI know that probably still counts as thoughts of death or dying. It’s just hard to explain because it doesn’t feel like I’m actively deciding anything. It’s more like the thought shows up, and then I feel guilty for even thinking it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your feelings of sadness or emptiness were significantly worse than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to separate it into neat chunks, because it hasn’t been like one bad weekend and then I bounced back. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. Then once school and work started slipping, it got a lot harder to pretend I was fine. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Like I can look at my sketchbook and remember that drawing used to be the thing I’d go to when I needed to clear my head. I’d sit there for hours and actually feel like myself again. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. That’s probably one of the bigger reasons I came in, even though it feels embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nI keep getting stuck on this idea that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m this disappointment people haven’t fully realized yet. Like with the apprenticeship, I feel like I’m kind of a fake there. Everyone else seems like they belong, like they know how to mess up and then just fix it and keep moving. But if I make a mistake, even something that other people would probably just correct and not think about again, I’ll replay it and turn it into proof that I shouldn’t be there.\n\nAnd it’s not just me feeling bad about myself quietly. I start thinking I’ve let my family down, because they were proud of me for getting into this and I’m supposed to be building something stable. And then I think about the younger apprentices too, which probably sounds dramatic, but I feel like I’m letting them down somehow, like I’m supposed to be showing I can handle it and instead I’m barely holding it together.\n\nSo, yeah… it’s not every single second, but it’s there a lot. This feeling that I’m failing people, or that I’ve already messed things up and everybody else is just being polite about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, pretty much every day it shows up in some form. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a disappointment to others every morning when you wake up?\n\nPatient: Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s timed perfectly like that. But mornings can definitely be when it starts, especially if I wake up and remember I have to go in to the apprenticeship or deal with people who are expecting me to be competent.\n\nIt’s like before anything has even happened, I’ll already be thinking, “What am I going to mess up today?” And then it turns into this bigger feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody somehow, or that I’m just pretending to belong there. Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.\n\nAnd then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nSo… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel worthless or like a disappointment to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. But then if I do one thing wrong, even something small that other people would probably just fix and move on from, it comes right back — that feeling that I’m a fake there, that I don’t actually belong, and that I’ve somehow ruined things for everybody.\n\nAnd it turns into this whole guilt thing, like I’m letting my family down because they were proud of me, and even letting the younger apprentices down, which I know sounds like a lot to put on myself. I used to always have it nearby, and even if I was tired I’d still doodle or plan something out, because it felt like mine, you know? Yeah… it has. I mean, I didn’t mark the exact day it started or anything, so I don’t want to pretend I have some perfect timeline, but this has definitely been going on for at least a couple weeks.

It’s been long enough that it’s not feeling like just one bad day after messing something up. It’s more like this depressed, heavy stretch has kind of been with me for… probably around nine months now.\n\nBut there were definitely times where it felt worse, or at least harder to keep hidden. At first I could sort of tell myself I was just tired or stressed and still push through enough that people maybe wouldn’t notice. That’s when it felt more obvious, like I couldn’t just cover it up by acting normal for a few hours.\n\nSo, yeah, the sadness or emptiness has been there for months, but it became more intense—or at least more impossible to manage quietly—when I started falling behind with school and work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really… or, I mean, I still know what I’m supposed to enjoy, if that makes sense. Lately I’ll walk right past it, or I’ll even pick it up and then just put it back down because there’s nothing there. It feels like too much effort, but also not even worth the effort.\n\nSame with volleyball. I used to look forward to it, even if I was tired, because once I got there I’d usually feel better and joke around with people. Now I’ll skip it and not even feel that disappointed. It’s just this blank feeling, like, “Okay, I’m not going.” And then I don’t really miss it the way I think I should.\n\nAnd I’ve been ignoring texts from the volunteer group too, which makes me feel bad because those people are nice and it used to matter to me. I used to like being part of something, having a reason to show up. Now the messages come in and I just stare at them, or don’t open them, and I don’t feel excited or useful or anything. It’s like those were the things that made me feel like me, and lately they’re just… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, honestly. Not always at the exact same level, but it’s there a lot.\n\nIt’s like I’ll do one thing wrong or forget something or need help with something at the apprenticeship, and instead of just thinking, “Okay, fix it and move on,” my brain turns it into this whole thing where I’m a fake and I shouldn’t be there. And I know other people probably mess up and just correct it, like it’s normal, but when it’s me it feels like proof that I’ve ruined things or that I’m disappointing everyone.\n\nAnd then it spreads past work too. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and how I’m letting them down, or even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things and instead I’m barely keeping up. So yeah… maybe “worthless” sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what it feels like some days. Mostly it’s this constant disappointment feeling, like I’ve already failed people and they just haven’t said it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a disappointment to others every morning when you wake up?\n\nPatient: Not every single morning the second I open my eyes, no. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s timed perfectly like that. But mornings can definitely be when it starts, especially if I wake up and remember I have to go in to the apprenticeship or deal with people who are expecting me to be competent.\n\nIt’s like before anything has even happened, I’ll already be thinking, “What am I going to mess up today?” And then it turns into this bigger feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody somehow, or that I’m just pretending to belong there. Like I’m a fake and everyone else is eventually going to notice.\n\nAnd then I think about my family being proud of me, and that makes it worse, because I feel like I’m letting them down. Or I’ll think about the younger apprentices too, which I know might sound like I’m putting too much weight on it, but I feel like I’m supposed to be proving I can handle this, and instead I’m getting stuck over mistakes that other people would probably just fix and move on from.\n\nSo… not literally every morning, but a lot of mornings, yeah, that disappointment feeling is already there pretty early.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel worthless or like a disappointment to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are some days where it’s quieter, I guess. I wouldn’t say it completely disappears in this clean way, like I wake up and feel confident and fine. It’s more like there are days where nothing really pokes at it as hard, so I can get through the day without spiraling on it the whole time.\n\nBut even on those days, it feels like it’s kind of sitting in the background. Like if I’m at the apprenticeship and something goes okay, or nobody has to correct me, then I can almost convince myself I’m not messing everything up. But then if I do one thing wrong, even something small that other people would probably just fix and move on from, it comes right back — that feeling that I’m a fake there, that I don’t actually belong, and that I’ve somehow ruined things for everybody.\n\nAnd it turns into this whole guilt thing, like I’m letting my family down because they were proud of me, and even letting the younger apprentices down, which I know sounds like a lot to put on myself. So… yes, I have better days, but I don’t know if I have many days where I feel totally free of it. It’s more like the volume gets turned down for a while.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually been one of the things that makes me feel weird, because it’s not like I decided to stop liking them, I just sort of… stopped reaching for them.\n\nLike my sketchbook, for example. I used to always have it nearby, and even if I was tired I’d still doodle or plan something out, because it felt like mine, you know? Like something that made me feel more like myself outside of work and everything. But lately I can literally walk past it sitting there and just not feel anything pulling me toward it. Not even guilt sometimes, just blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too. I used to look forward to playing, even just casually, because it got me out of my head. Now if there’s a chance to go, I’ll skip it or tell myself I’m too tired or it’s too much effort, but it’s not only being tired. It’s more like I can’t imagine enjoying it enough to make myself go.\n\nSame with the volunteer group texts. Those used to make me feel connected, like I was doing something good and being part of something. Now I see the messages pop up and I just ignore them, and I don’t even have a strong feeling about it in the moment. It’s kind of scary how little I feel about things that used to matter to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless or like you're a disappointment to others every single day?\n\nPatient: It feels close to every day, yeah. I don’t know if I’d say every single day in the exact same way or at the exact same level, because some days I can keep moving and not get stuck on it as hard. But it’s there a lot.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I keep getting this feeling like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when the thing that happened is probably something small that other people would just fix and move on from. At the apprenticeship, especially, I’ll make a mistake or even think I might have made one, and then my head goes straight to, “I’m a fake, I don’t belong here, everyone’s going to realize I shouldn’t be doing this.”\n\nAnd then it spreads out past just work. I start thinking about my family and how they were proud of me, and it feels like I’m letting them down. And even with the younger apprentices, I feel like I’m supposed to be someone who can handle things or set a decent example, and instead I feel like I’m failing them too. I know that probably sounds like I’m making myself responsible for way too much, but in the moment it feels really true. So… maybe not every single day perfectly, but most days, yeah, that worthless-disappointment feeling is there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of worthlessness or being a disappointment to others usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually it’s not just like a quick thought that pops up and then leaves. It can hang around for a lot of the day, especially if something at the apprenticeship sets it off early.\n\nLike if I mess something up, or even just think I did, it’s not over once the actual mistake is fixed. Other people might just correct it and move on, but I’ll keep replaying it in my head for hours. I’ll be working and still thinking, “You don’t belong here, you’re faking it, everyone’s going to figure out you shouldn’t be here.” And then it turns into this bigger thing where I feel like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even when I know logically that sounds dramatic.\n\nIt’s not always at full volume the entire day, I guess. Sometimes I’m distracted enough to get through tasks. But it’s like it stays underneath everything. I’ll think about my family being proud of me and feel like I’m letting them down, and then I think about the younger apprentices too, like I’m supposed to be someone steady or useful and instead I’m just disappointing people.\n\nSo… yeah, most of the day a lot of the time. Maybe not every single minute, but it follows me around once it’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of worthlessness or being a disappointment to others lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, I didn’t mark the exact day it started or anything, so I don’t want to pretend I have some perfect timeline, but this has definitely been going on for at least a couple weeks.\n\nIt’s been long enough that it’s not feeling like just one bad day after messing something up. It keeps coming back — that feeling that I’ve ruined things for everybody, or that I’m basically a fake in the apprenticeship and everyone’s going to figure it out. And then I start thinking about my family being proud of me and it feels like I’m letting them down, and even the younger apprentices, like I’m supposed to be someone they can look up to a little or at least trust, and instead I feel like I’m failing them too.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is, some of the stuff that sets it off is probably something other people would just fix and move on from. There are some days where it’s not most of the day, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just sitting there every single day from morning to night thinking I’m worthless. Some days I’m busy enough, or things go smoothly enough, that it’s more in the background or it only hits me for a while.

But even on the better days, it doesn’t feel totally gone. It’s more like… if nothing goes wrong, I can kind of function around it. But if I make a mistake at the apprenticeship, or even think I might have, it can come back really fast. Then I’m right back to feeling like I ruined things for everybody, like I’m a fake and I don’t belong there, and like my family was proud of me for no reason because I’m letting them down. And I think about the younger apprentices too, like I should be someone they can trust or learn from, and instead I feel like I’m just disappointing them.

So yes, there are days where it’s not taking up most of the day. But lately, a lot of days it does take over, or it’s close enough that it still feels like it’s following me around.

Like something that made me feel more like myself outside of work and everything. But lately I can literally walk past it sitting there and just not feel anything pulling me toward it. Not even guilt sometimes, just blank.\n\nAnd volleyball too. I used to look forward to playing, even just casually, because it got me out of my head. Now I see the messages pop up and I just ignore them, and I don’t even have a strong feeling about it in the moment. I know that probably sounds like I’m making myself responsible for way too much, but in the moment it feels really true. So… maybe not every single day perfectly, but most days, yeah, that worthless-disappointment feeling is there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of worthlessness or being a disappointment to others usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually it’s not just like a quick thought that pops up and then leaves. It can hang around for a lot of the day, especially if something at the apprenticeship sets it off early.\n\nLike if I mess something up, or even just think I did, it’s not over once the actual mistake is fixed. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… a lot of the time it feels like it’s just kind of there underneath whatever I’m doing. Like I can be doing normal work stuff, measuring something, hauling materials, listening to someone explain what needs to be done, and part of me is still thinking, “You messed everything up,” or “You’re not really supposed to be here.”

It’s especially tied to the apprenticeship. That’s really what made me come in, honestly — I keep getting stuck on this feeling like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even over things I know other people would probably just fix and move on from. Like if something doesn’t go perfectly, my brain turns it into proof that I’m a fake there, like I somehow tricked people into thinking I could do this.

And then it spreads out, I guess. It’s not just about the task anymore. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and feeling like I’m letting them down, and then the younger apprentices too, like I should be someone steady or helpful and instead I’m just disappointing them.

So yes, even when I’m doing everyday stuff, it can still be there. Maybe not always screaming at me every second, but it’s like background noise that keeps coming back.

Yeah… it does. Not every single task, and I can still get through some basic things, but it’s definitely affecting my work and the apprenticeship stuff.

Like, I’ve had instructors and my foreman have to come back and chase me about unfinished sketches or things I said I’d get done and then I just… I don’t know, I get stuck. I’ll start something, then I’ll second-guess it, or I’ll feel like it’s already not good enough, and then it takes me way longer than it should. And then being behind makes the whole “I’m disappointing everybody” thing worse, so it turns into this stupid loop where I’m slower because I feel awful, and then I feel more awful because I’m slower.

It’s embarrassing because these are things I worked really hard to be able to do. I don’t want anyone thinking I don’t care or that I’m lazy, because I do care — probably too much — but I can tell it’s starting to show. When my foreman has to remind me about something I should’ve already finished, I feel like I’m damaging the apprenticeship, like I’m getting closer to messing up the certification I worked so hard for. That scares me a lot.

So yes, it interferes. I’m still trying to push through, but it’s affecting how quickly I finish things and whether I finish them without people having to check up on me.

Then I’m right back to feeling like I ruined things for everybody, like I’m a fake and I don’t belong there, and like my family was proud of me for no reason because I’m letting them down. And I think about the younger apprentices too, like I should be someone they can trust or learn from, and instead I feel like I’m just disappointing them.\n\nSo yes, there are days where it’s not taking up most of the day. But lately, a lot of days it does take over, or it’s close enough that it still feels like it’s following me around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worthlessness or disappointment feelings are always there, even when you're doing everyday tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot of the time it feels like it’s just kind of there underneath whatever I’m doing. Like I can be doing normal work stuff, measuring something, hauling materials, listening to someone explain what needs to be done, and part of me is still thinking, “You messed everything up,” or “You’re not really supposed to be here.”\n\nIt’s especially tied to the apprenticeship. That’s really what made me come in, honestly — I keep getting stuck on this feeling like I’ve ruined things for everybody, even over things I know other people would probably just fix and move on from. Like if something doesn’t go perfectly, my brain turns it into proof that I’m a fake there, like I somehow tricked people into thinking I could do this.\n\nAnd then it spreads out, I guess. It’s not just about the task anymore. I start thinking about my family being proud of me and feeling like I’m letting them down, and then the younger apprentices too, like I should be someone steady or helpful and instead I’m just disappointing them.\n\nSo yes, even when I’m doing everyday stuff, it can still be there. Maybe not always screaming at me every second, but it’s like background noise that keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your feelings of worthlessness or being a disappointment to others interfere with your ability to complete daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. Not every single task, and I can still get through some basic things, but it’s definitely affecting my work and the apprenticeship stuff.\n\nLike, I’ve had instructors and my foreman have to come back and chase me about unfinished sketches or things I said I’d get done and then I just… I don’t know, I get stuck. I’ll start something, then I’ll second-guess it, or I’ll feel like it’s already not good enough, and then it takes me way longer than it should. And then being behind makes the whole “I’m disappointing everybody” thing worse, so it turns into this stupid loop where I’m slower because I feel awful, and then I feel more awful because I’m slower.\n\nIt’s embarrassing because these are things I worked really hard to be able to do. I don’t want anyone thinking I don’t care or that I’m lazy, because I do care — probably too much — but I can tell it’s starting to show. When my foreman has to remind me about something I should’ve already finished, I feel like I’m damaging the apprenticeship, like I’m getting closer to messing up the certification I worked so hard for. That scares me a lot.\n\nSo yes, it interferes. I’m still trying to push through, but it’s affecting how quickly I finish things and whether I finish them without people having to check up on me."
  }
